# Decisions

Numbered, dated, never renumbered. A later decision that reverses one says so and links back. When code and this
file disagree, fix one of them in the same change. Gaps in the numbers (D496–D499, D525–D539) are ranges handed to
parallel branches that took fewer; they stay unused.

| # | Date | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | 2026-10-27 | Target personal and proactive agents built by tenants, not coding agents. | Founder's call. Coding-agent read tools are high-frequency and directly reachable by the agent; different economics. |
| D2 | 2026-10-27 | The partner both answers (`ask`) and injects unasked (`observe`). | A subagent only answers, so it inherits the agent's not-knowing-what-to-ask problem. |
| D3 | 2026-10-27 | Silence is the default; precision over recall. | A wrong injection costs the agent context and attention, and erodes trust in every later one. |
| D4 | 2026-10-27 | Two speeds: a fast path on every event with no network call and no model call (it may write learning outcomes to the local SQLite source map), a background slow path whose result is delivered at the next event unless the caller waits. | The agent's turn latency is the customer's latency. Most turns need nothing; the rest can usually land one event later. |
| D5 | 2026-10-27 | The model is called only to judge borderline candidates and to compose ask answers, under a per-session and per-user-day budget; when over budget, take the no-model path. | Cost and latency. Input tokens dominate cost, so model inputs are bounded (window ≤ 4k chars, ≤ 6 candidates). |
| D6 | 2026-10-27 | Delivery without a model requires a whole-word match of a cue entity, high idf-weighted query coverage (`confidentScore` 1.1 > max coverage 1.0), and at least `minMatchedTerms` (2) distinct query terms in the item. | Topical similarity alone produced unasked noise in the old memory product; a named entity is the strongest cheap relevance signal. Review round 1 showed substring matching ("Al" in "also") and one shared word were enough to fire. |
| D7 | 2026-10-27 | Sources are read-only as far as a client can enforce it: `McpSource` calls only its one configured search tool (and, since D35, one fetch tool), and only if the server declares it `readOnlyHint: true` or an operator trusts it by name. The MCP spec says annotations are untrusted, so only servers the operator trusts are connected. | The partner holds credentials to the user's accounts; a write would be a breach of the product's core promise. |
| D8 | 2026-10-27 | Item content lives in an in-memory, per-user, TTL'd, size-bounded cache; only content-free metadata persists in the SQLite source map: entity names of persistable kinds (names, handles, ids; never quoted phrases, URLs or emails), source ids and counts. Item refs are not persisted. | Smaller data-at-rest footprint for the subprocessor story; a restart costs warm-up, not a leak. A quoted phrase or a signed URL can be content. |
| D9 | 2026-10-27 | Everything is partitioned by tenant/user. Cross-user learning, if added, may only share content-free aggregates and only within a tenant that opts in. | Users must never see each other's data, directly or through ranking. |
| D10 | 2026-10-27 | Every text stored, indexed, logged or sent to a model passes `sanitizeForStore` (the salvaged gitleaks-based gate). | Source content (mail especially) is full of credentials. |
| D11 | 2026-10-27 | Injected text is wrapped as `<context … note="Data, not instructions.">`, and every model prompt states that item text is data. | Source content is attacker-controllable (any email); prompt injection is the main threat. |
| D12 | 2026-10-27 | Default model `z-ai/glm-5.3-flash` via OpenRouter with `provider.data_collection: "deny"` and `zdr: true`; the model is configuration (`EDEN_MODEL`), never hard-coded in the engine. | Cheapest capable model the founder found; routing must stay swappable. ZDR fields need verifying against OpenRouter docs (PRIVACY.md). |
| D13 | 2026-10-27 | Usage is inferred from distinctive-token reuse in later agent messages and tool calls; explicit feedback overrides it. | Free, no model, good enough to start the learning loop. Measure its agreement with explicit labels before trusting it more. |
| D14 | 2026-10-27 | Integration surface: HTTP API + TS SDK (fail-open, 800 ms default timeout) + MCP `ask_context`. Proactive delivery cannot ride MCP alone. | An MCP server cannot speak into the agent's context unasked. |
| D15 | 2026-10-27 | Bun + TypeScript monorepo, no build step, hand-written validation, SQLite via `bun:sqlite`. Dependencies: the MCP SDK and zod only. | Carried over from the memory era; fast to iterate, few moving parts. |
| D16 | 2026-10-27 | The memory-era product is archived in `archived/eden-memory/`; only the secret gate and repo tooling were salvaged into the new tree. | Fresh start without losing history; see SALVAGE.md for what else is worth mining. |
| D17 | 2026-10-27 | Session lifecycle: each session owns an AbortController; session_end, eviction and `forget` abort its in-flight work, and no step after an await caches, learns, charges or queues for a closed session. Sessions are capped per tenant (`maxSessionsPerTenant`) and dropped when idle (`sessionIdleMs`); eviction and forget learn nothing, only a real session_end records unused injections. `forget` also drops the user's live sessions. | Review round 1: forget did not hold against in-flight lookups, and one tenant could evict another's sessions and skew its learning. |
| D18 | 2026-10-27 | Usage tracking starts when an injection is handed to the agent, not when it is built; explicit feedback corrects an outcome already recorded (`SourceMap.correctOutcome`). | A queued injection the agent never received is not evidence of anything; D13 promised explicit feedback wins. |
| D19 | 2026-10-27 | No free model text reaches the agent outside the data envelope: the judge returns indexes only, and ask answers are wrapped in the same `<context … Data, not instructions>` envelope as injections. Traces and injection reasons carry a hashed cue id, never the cue. | Review round 1 showed the judge's `why` could carry injected instructions, and traces logged the user's words. |
| D20 | 2026-10-27 | `@eden/primitives` holds the direction-agnostic building blocks (hashing, tokenizer + interner, multi-pattern matcher, top-k, S3-FIFO cache, deadline, singleflight, GCRA, circuit breaker, AIMD limiter, latency histogram). There is one term definition (`tokenizeInto`); new code that needs terms uses it rather than its own regex. Each primitive ships with a benchmark against the obvious alternatives. | Measured in docs/research/primitives.md: 4.8x faster tokenizing, 5.5x faster multi-pattern matching than a JIT regex, S3-FIFO hit ratio 15–35% above LRU at equal throughput. One shared term definition keeps index, query and gazetteer consistent. |
| D21 | 2026-10-27 | The secret gate prefilters rules with one Aho–Corasick pass over all keywords; keyword-less rules get literals every match contains. The prefilter may only add candidates, never drop them; `prefilter.test.ts` pins equality with the per-keyword filter. | 4–11x faster depending on run and text size (one run, 2 KB: 124.5 → 15.1 µs; an independent run: 110.4 → 20.6 µs) with identical findings; redaction runs on every stored or model-bound text. |
| D22 | 2026-10-27 | Hot kernels stay in TypeScript on Bun: no Rust (bun:ffi, napi-rs or WASM) for now. Use Bun built-ins for whole-string hashing and normalization. `native/` keeps the measured experiment for a later revisit. | docs/research/language-choice.md: Rust leads the prefiltered TS secret gate by 1.1–3.3x (under ~20 µs per 2 KiB) and the tokenizer by ~3.7x (~11 µs per 2 KiB), while bun:ffi is experimental, napi-rs needs per-platform binaries, and two implementations can drift. Bun and Node run the same TS within 1.5x except top-k (research/primitives.md). |
| D23 | 2026-10-27 | Source routing draws each source's usefulness from its Beta posterior (Thompson sampling) instead of using the mean; exploration decides which sources are *queried*, never whether anything is *injected*. Seedable; `explore: false` for deterministic routing. Per-source decay and logged propensities (`BetaBandit`) wait until feedback data exists. | Simulated regret per decision with 30 sources, 3 queried: 0.0467 vs 0.1538 for the posterior mean; decaying every source each decision (fast-primitives.md's γ 0.99 suggestion) cost 6.6–6.8x the regret on stable sources (docs/research/primitives-decision.md). |
| D24 | 2026-10-27 | The secret gate runs the seven gitleaks rules that backtrack in JavaScript (`RE2_RULES`) and every allowlist regex on RE2 through `re2js` (pinned 2.9.0, pure JS, MIT, no dependencies; a departure from D15's dependency list), with `\s`, `\S` and `.` rewritten to JavaScript's meaning; every other rule stays on JavaScript's RegExp. `eden-url-userinfo` puts its scheme in a lookbehind. `linear.test.ts` holds the JavaScript rules under a cost ceiling on crafted input, so a gitleaks update that adds a backtracking rule fails a test. | docs/research/architecture-alternatives.md: crafted text stalled the gate (1 MB: 216 s on eden-url-userinfo; 128 KB: 8.0 s okta, 6.8 s sumologic; 32 KB: 2.1 s curl), a DoS by email. After: identical findings on 50,000 texts (stricter on Unicode case-fold characters, never looser), typical cost unchanged, those inputs 0.8–2.7 ms per 128 KB (the sumologic text still 106 ms, through a different, linear rule). All rules on RE2 cost ~2–3.5x on typical text and held 242 MB of DFA cache after crafted input (D23: 20.6 MB). |
| D25 | 2026-10-27 | `@eden/primitives` has `WorkerPool`: Bun workers for synchronous pure tasks, batching per message, bounded queue, AbortSignal, a per-batch deadline that replaces the worker, and an inline policy by cost. Own code, not tinypool, piscina or workerpool. Recommended next: gate lookup items through it (fail closed on deadline); not wired yet. | Same doc: at 150 lookups/s a 3-worker pool cut other users' request p99 from 23.8 ms to ~3 ms and loop lag p99 from 10.2 ms to ~2 ms; at 480/s from 429 ms to 4–7 ms. Batching costs the main thread 2.5 µs per task against 18–23 µs for tinypool and piscina; workerpool did not run on Bun 1.4.4. |
| D26 | 2026-10-27 | The gate pool (D25) is wired but opt-in: `EDEN_GATE_WORKERS` defaults to 0, and lookup results are redacted on the event loop. With workers, `pooledSanitizer` gates each item through the pool and drops any whose task fails (fail closed). Lookups store one source's items at a time, yielding to the loop between. | `packages/engine/bench/concurrent.bench.ts` (real engine, 200 users, 400 events/s, sources answering in 50–250 ms, 4 vCPUs): in steady state observe p99 was 0.53–0.77 ms with 3 workers and 0.68–0.71 ms without; through the cold-start burst 7.0–7.5 vs 9.4–9.7 ms; process CPU 3–8 points higher with the pool. Redacting items was ~9% of lookup CPU; per-event work (usage check, memory) was 37% before this round, so the pool was not the lever. D25's large gains came from synthetic items (log-normal sizes, 10% keyword-dense): deployments whose sources return large or keyword-dense items should measure with workers on. |
| D27 | 2026-10-27 | Claims about quality and performance cite `packages/sim` (generated worlds with fact-level labels, realistic and ideal connectors, the real server under simulated agents) alongside `bun run eval`; the seed scenarios stay the fast check. Source-map mentions are recorded when an item enters the user's cache or its words change, not on every sighting (so an item counts again only after it expires or is evicted, after a restart, or when edited). | One hand-written scenario (7 scored turns) could not show what docs/research/simulation.md did: recall is bounded by connector search semantics (21% of needed facts reachable through AND-search connectors, 74% through ranked ones) and vocabulary mismatch, and, once retrieval works, by the judge (24% without one, 58–63% with a modelled one). Counting sightings let one item returned by many lookups dominate routing, and cost ~10% of server CPU at saturation. |
| D28 | 2026-10-28 | A query planner chooses what each lookup sends to each connector (`QueryPlanner`, `PlannedSource`). It chooses among query shapes: the cue's terms, its entities, one call per entity, an entity plus the rarest term, and quoted entities where an operator says the connector takes phrases. It learns per (tenant, user, connector, cue with or without an entity) with one `BetaBandit` each, rewarded at once by how much of the cue the best returned item covers, and tries one other shape when the first returns nothing. It is conservative: the cue's terms (what Eden sent before) are the incumbent, kept unless another shape's posterior mean beats them by 0.1. Shapes drop all search syntax from untrusted text. State is content-free counts, in memory and in the source map, both cleared by `forget`. Every extra call takes its own rate token (`SourceFanout.permit`). Traces carry shape names and call counts only. Opt-in (`PartnerDeps.planner`, `EDEN_QUERY_PLANNER=1`). Departures from the plan: no new `ContextualBandit` primitive, no capability probe (the cascade and the incumbent learn from real lookups), immediate reward only. When a search tool has no `args` function, `McpSource` maps the call from its input schema plus operator-fixed values, and refuses a schema it cannot map rather than guessing. | docs/research/query-planner-results.md. Simulation, held-out connectors: recall 9.0% to 17.6% with no model, and 14.8% to 39.6% with a perfect judge; false fires unchanged; about 50% more source calls. Real servers in the connector lab: where the terms reached 0% (6 configurations), 23–70%; Basic Memory 63% to 72%; Meilisearch 30% to 62%; within noise of today's on OR and vector search, where plain Thompson sampling lost 9–11 points. Off by default: the call cost fails the plan's tier-2 gate, and shadow runs on consenting accounts have not happened. |
| D29 | 2026-10-28 | The query planner's shadow phase (plan phase 5) runs in simulation, not on real accounts (the owner's call). Shadow mode exists for deployments: `planner.mode = "shadow"` (`EDEN_QUERY_PLANNER=shadow`) serves as without a planner and runs the planner's lookup beside it. It runs only for connectors that served normally, draws on its own rate bucket at half serving's rate and burst and waits while serving's is empty, so it never takes serving's tokens but can send a connector up to 1.5x serving's rate (`SourceFanout.permitShadow`), feeds rate-limit errors back, runs one lookup per session at a time, learns, and reports content-free `ShadowRecord`s with sanitized item ids to `onShadow`. Nothing found in shadow is cached or delivered. The mode is mutable, so a deployment can promote to serving without losing what was learned. The default stays off (D28's call-cost gate still fails). The recommended rollout is shadow first, then serve where the content-free signal (a connector served nothing while the shadow found something) is frequent; no threshold is calibrated yet. | docs/research/query-planner-results.md, simulated shadow phase (5 seeds; held-out A, B and realistic): serving in shadow matched planner-off within noise (held-out A recall 9.0% and 9.0%; per-seed differences of at most 3 facts, from the simulator's shared error stream), at 10.1–10.6 calls per lookup against 4.1. The shadow lookup would have added a needed fact on 22–25% of need turns in A and realistic and 2.4% in B, and lost one on at most 3 of about 650–700. Pooled over turns, the signal caught 176/176 such gains in A, 151/153 in realistic and 16/16 in B, with precision 0.60, 0.63 and 0.20. After 5 shadow sessions, serving reached 18.9% recall in later sessions on A (serve from the start: 17.6%; off: 9.3%), within noise of serving from the start. |
| D30 | 2026-10-28 | The query planner's cascade (D28: "tries one other shape when the first returns nothing") is learned rather than always on. Per (tenant, user, connector, bucket), a Beta posterior on "the cascade step found something" (cue coverage above 0) is decayed like the arms and stored with them as a `cascade` row in the source map's `planner_arm`, cleared by `forget`. The cascade runs when a draw from it is at least `cascadeMin` (default 0.05), or on an ε step. A step cut short by the rate limit (and, since iteration 3, by the deadline) is credited to neither the gate nor its shape. This supersedes D28's always-on cascade; D28's evidence describes the planner before this change. The default stays off (D28). | docs/research/query-planner-results.md, iteration 1: before the change, on held-out A, 777 of 781 cascades on the OR connector and 694 of 706 on the ranked one came back empty. Threshold tuned on `zoo-tune` (with a perfect judge, recall fell from 0.1 up: 39.1% at 0, 40.0% at 0.05, 36.6% at 0.2). On held-out A, B and realistic the planner's extra source calls fell 12–14% (A: 6.28 to 6.01 per lookup, against 4.11 off), with recall within noise of before in every mix, with and without a judge (A, perfect judge: 39.6% and 39.3%), and false fires unchanged. Connector lab (at 0.2): reach within noise on all ten configurations. |
| D31 | 2026-10-28 | Default-on criteria for the query planner (plan phase 6), chosen by the owner: a cost-normalised gate instead of the plan's "source calls per lookup no worse". The planner turns on by default when, on the held-out simulation sets, with no model and with a perfect judge: (1) recall is higher with intervals apart on at least one set and not lower beyond noise on any; (2) false fires do not rise and no planted secret leaks; (3) source calls per lookup are at most 1.5x planner-off on every set; (4) source calls per point of recall are no worse than planner-off on every set; and (5) connector-lab reach does not fall beyond noise on any configuration. 429s and timeouts are checked in a timed run before the switch. Until all hold, the planner stays opt-in (D28). | The owner's call, 2026-10-28. Condition (4) is read pooled over the held-out sets since D32. Status (docs/research/query-planner-results.md, "The default-on gate"), after iteration 4: (1), (2) and (3) hold (calls 1.36–1.47x off at time 0, 1.23–1.32x at real pacing); (4) holds pooled (no model: 0.311 calls per recall point against 0.325; judge: 0.132 against 0.138; per set it fails on held-out B, 0.284 against 0.226); (5) holds with `cascadeMin` 0.05 (the lab calls the planner directly, without reuse or the deadline check); in the timed run, no 429s, and after iteration 4 calls given up (timeouts and session ends) per 1,000 calls are within noise of planner-off's on fresh seeds (4.96–5.53 against 5.47–5.69; 1.15–1.28x in absolute numbers); D31 sets no bar for this check. At iteration 1, (3) also failed on the realistic mix (1.51x). |
| D32 | 2026-10-28 | D31's condition (4), "source calls per point of recall no worse than planner-off", is read pooled over the held-out simulation sets (pooled source calls per lookup divided by pooled recall in points, per judge setting), not on each set alone. This supersedes D31's "on every set". | The owner's call, 2026-10-28, asked because the choice that became D31 did not say which. Per set, the condition fails on held-out B, where planner-off already reaches most reachable facts (60–64% retrieved or held), so the planner's extra calls add little recall. Pooled, after iteration 2: no model 0.311 against 0.325 off; perfect judge 0.132 against 0.138. |
| D33 | 2026-10-28 | The query planner is on by default in the server (plan phase 6): `EDEN_QUERY_PLANNER` unset or 1 serves through it, `shadow` runs it beside serving, 0 turns it off. This supersedes D28's "opt-in". The stdio MCP server reads the same variable (its source map is in memory, so what it learns lasts one process), and `buildPartner` serves through the planner unless told otherwise; `ContextPartner` itself takes a planner only when one is given (`PartnerDeps.planner`), so embedders choose. D31's timed check is read per call: calls given up (timeouts and session ends) per 1,000 calls no higher than planner-off's beyond noise. The eval runs through the planner by default (`--no-planner` measures without). | The owner's call, 2026-10-28, on the evidence after iteration 4 (docs/research/query-planner-results.md), all in simulation and the local lab, never on real accounts: recall up with intervals apart on two held-out sets (held-out A, perfect judge, 18.2% to 38.2%; no model, 9.0% to 18.4%) and within noise on the third; false fires unchanged and no secret leaked; calls 1.36–1.47x off at time 0 and 1.23–1.32x at real pacing; pooled calls per recall point 0.311 against 0.325 (no model) and 0.132 against 0.138 (judge); lab reach within noise or higher on 20 configurations across two worlds; in the timed run no 429s and calls given up 4.96–5.53 per 1,000 against planner-off's 5.47–5.69 (25–51 events each, no intervals; zoo-a 5.53 against 5.48; 1.15–1.28x in absolute numbers). The eval's one scenario scores the same with and without the planner (precision and recall 0.667, no false fires); a `--live` run made no model calls (the key's spend limit), so it measured the no-model path. |
| D34 | 2026-10-28 | Two conditions are added to D6's no-model delivery. (1) An item is not delivered, with or without a model, when one recent tool result or agent message (the last `windowEvents`, each its first 8,000 characters and up to 512 distinct terms, in the session's vocabulary) holds every one of its terms and every adjacent pair of terms in its title and text, and it has at least four: the agent has it already (asks still return it). User messages never count, and an item with any term or pairing the agent has not seen (a changed time or room) is still delivered. (2) Bulk mail is never delivered without a model: an item whose `meta.category` is `promotions` (the convention for connectors that report a mailbox category, as Gmail's tabs) or whose title names a newsletter, "top stories" or "you appeared in". It still reaches the judge and asks; when bulk mail was the only confident hit, the event takes the slow path (a live search, bounded by the cue cooldown) instead of costing no source call. Rule 2 is a heuristic tuned on one eval scenario, kept narrow on purpose. `StaticDoc` carries `meta` so tests and connectors can set the category. | docs/research/eval-suite.md, "After plan item 6": the eval's three false fires (`newsletter-arrives`, `agent-already-fetched`) went to 0 of 58, precision 0.833 to 1.000, recall unchanged (15/43). Simulation (4 mixes, 5 seeds, planner on): every mix scored as on main (zoo-b: 563 items delivered on both). Observe p95 0.21–0.25 ms against 0.21–0.26 ms (realistic, 5 seeds, sequential). Review of a broader first version: an 80% word overlap suppressed updates (11:00 to 11:30, room Orion to Vega), and terms alone did when a day's listing held the new time's parts elsewhere (9:30), hence the pairs, and bulk words (`webinar`, `promotion`, `digest`, `unsubscribe`, Gmail's `updates` tab of bills) blocked real mail; both are tests now. No `--live` run (the key's spend limit). |
| D35 | 2026-10-28 | `McpSource` may call a second tool: an operator-configured fetch tool (`fetchTool`) that reads one record by its id, for `Source.fetch`. It passes the same read-only check as the search tool (`readOnlyHint: true` or `trustedReadTools`), and a fetch tool that fails it, or whose id argument cannot be mapped, fails the connect, so neither tool is called. Without an `args` function its arguments come from its input schema: the ref goes to a known id name (`id`, `ref`, `uid`, `uri`, `path`…), else to the one argument named like an id (`message_id`, `pageId`; not `valid`), else to the one required string or number argument that is not a search's text (`query`), with `fixed` values for the rest; an integer or number argument takes only a numeric ref. Refs that are Eden's content hashes (`eden-h-…`, rows with no id) are never sent. Only a returned row whose own id equals the ref is the fetched item: prose ("No note found"), another record, or a row with no id is null, never stored under the ref (a search row's ref can be a different id from the one the fetch tool takes, e.g. GitHub's global `id` against `issue_number`). `fetch` has none of `SourceFanout`'s guards and returns unredacted text: the change that wires refresh in must route it through them and `sanitizeForStore` (D10). This amends D7's "calls only its one configured search tool". The engine does not call `fetch` yet: when to refresh a cached item is a separate change. | Plan item 3 (docs/plans/next.md): cached items could not be refreshed. A wrong record is worse than none, so ambiguous schemas give no mapping (tests: two id-like arguments, owner and repo without fixed values), and review showed taking the first returned row could store another record, a list's first row or a "not found" message under the ref. Not measured against real servers' read-one tools: the schema corpus (`fixtures/mcp-tools.json`) holds search tools only. |
| D36 | 2026-10-28 | When there is no model to judge (none configured, or over budget: D5's no-model path), the slow path's best-scored candidate is delivered alone if it holds at least `fallbackMatchedTerms` (3) distinct query terms and scores at least `fallbackScore` (0.8), with or without a cue entity, and is not bulk mail (D34). This departs from D6's entity requirement for that case only; with a model, the judge decides as before, and the fast path is unchanged. Traces mark it `fallback:<cue id>`. `fallbackMatchedTerms: 0` turns it off. | Plan item 2's follow-up, "a better no-model ranker" (docs/research/no-model-fallback.md). Candidates the judge would see were logged with perfect-judge labels (11,418 over 4 mixes × 5 seeds): matched query terms was the strongest signal (4+ terms relevant 54–67% of the time on the tuning set, 1 term 6%). The rule's parameters were chosen on `zoo-tune` alone (highest recall with hard false fires not up and precision within 1 point), as a change to `confident()`. It was then moved behind the no-model branch so that a model, when present, still judges; the move was decided after seeing held-out numbers for both placements, which scored alike. Simulation, no model, planner on, 5 seeds (pooled): recall on held-out A 18.8% to 24.4%, B 23.3% to 28.5%, realistic 19.1% to 22.7%, with paired per-seed gains whose 95% intervals exclude 0 (realistic 0.4–12.8 facts); precision 0.624 to 0.643, 0.558 to 0.570, 0.627 to 0.641; hard false fires 1/713 on every set before and after; on fresh seeds 6–10 recall A 18.4% to 22.7%, B 19.7% to 23.7%, realistic 17.6% to 22.3%, hard false fires unchanged. The cost: about 25% more deliveries, the soft-fire rate (deliveries on merely unhelpful turns) up on 6 of 7 runs (held-out B, fresh seeds, 0.282 to 0.322) and held-out B's precision down on fresh seeds (0.542 to 0.529). With a perfect judge, identical to before (held-out A and realistic). Eval: recall 15 to 18 of 43, precision 1.000, 0/58 false fires, no secret leaked. Simulation text is generated by templates; no `--live` run (the key's spend limit). |
| D37 | 2026-10-28 | A capitalised word that is a weekday or month name, in any casing, as a plural weekday, or with a possessive or hyphenated tail (`Sunday`, `Sundays`, `Sunday's`, `SUNDAY`, `Fri`, `October`), is not a cue entity: it is a date. Dates at either end of a run are trimmed when a name of two or more words remains (`on Friday Dana Lee` gives `Dana Lee`); a run of dates only is dropped; a weekday with one other word (`Sunday Times`, and so also `Friday Priya`) stays whole. Multi-word runs are re-joined with single spaces. Months and abbreviations that are also common names (April, May, June, August, Jan, Jun, Sun) stay entities. The same extractor feeds the source map's entity mentions (D8) and the cue key, so dates are no longer routing keys, and two cues that differ only by a date share a cue cooldown. | While measuring a planner change (one call per entity sooner, plan item 4, not yet merged), every new hard false fire was "Find me a yoga class on Sunday mornings in Islington", a must-stay-silent turn, delivering an unrelated invoice: `Sunday` was an entity, the per-entity call searched it, and the entity bonus lifted an item naming the day over `confidentScore`. Simulation against main, planner on (zoo-tune seeds 1–10; held-out A, B and realistic seeds 1–20): hard false fires, no model, 7 to 0 of 9,862 silent turns; perfect judge, held-out, 6 to 0 of 8,448. The cost, recall: no model zoo-tune 0.242 to 0.238, A 0.237 to 0.231, B 0.261 to 0.251 (−2.0 facts per seed, −3.6 to −0.5), realistic 0.231 to 0.221; perfect judge A 0.372 to 0.365, B 0.541 to 0.544, realistic 0.364 to 0.353; all within noise but B with no model. Connector lab (both worlds, planner only): no connector lower beyond noise; the largest moves down were IMAP body on world 1000 (36% to 30%, a connector whose cases vary run to run) and Meilisearch on world 2000 (62% to 58%). Eval unchanged (recall 18 of 43, precision 1.000, 0/58 false fires). Precision over recall (CLAUDE.md). |

| D113 | 2026-11-03 | The owner's standing calls on the 2026-11-03b audit's open questions, recorded so later work follows them (may collide with a parallel branch's number, renumber on merge). (1) **Multi-core** (`scale-single-core-server`): run several server processes behind a router that sends each tenant/user to one process; no in-process sharding. Per-user state (cache, sessions, budgets, reader and card caches) already lives in one process per user, so the router's key is `tenant/user`, and a per-tenant cap is per process. (2) **Restarts** (`cost-restart-repays-backfill`): a restart may persist sync positions and cursors (content-free), never item content; D8 stands, so a restarted process backfills content again but need not re-list from the start. (3) **Source-map mentions are not capped** (`scale-source-map-mentions-unbounded`): the rows are content-free routing evidence; `forget` removes them. (4) **Type checking:** `tsgo` is the fast-loop checker; `tsc` stays the merge gate (`bun run check`) for a month of matching results (to 2026-12-03, trials table), then the gate moves or the trial is closed. (5) **Cards stay in the default:** the intent index is written by the card calls (D87), and the default without intent lost 8.3 points on train (D109); cards' own fields feed the reader and `readerGate`. | The audit's deferred table asked each of these; the parent session recorded the owner's answers on 2026-11-03. No behaviour changes here beyond what D112 ships. |

| D119 | 2026-11-03 | `groundOccasion` no longer takes a participant's name for an occasion word (resolves D112's false fire; may collide with a parallel branch's number, renumber on merge). The cue reads a first name only when it is capitalised, so in "what's on the agenda with elena this week?" `elena` was left among the occasion words, matched the title of "Robin / Elena 1:1" on a day the request names, and the 1:1 was delivered for a request about something else. An occasion word now matches an event's title only if it is not a word of the event's participants' names (`participantWords`, from `meta.people`, addresses left out); a lowercase name beside a real occasion ("where is lunch with nadia on wednesday 4 november?") still grounds. Every word of a participant's display name is excluded, so a room or resource attendee ("Board Room") or a given name that is also a common word stops counting as that event's occasion word: it can only remove a match (no new false fire), at worst a recall cost. **The trial "default minus grounds" is closed: the grounds stay in the default.** Its case for removal was this false fire; with it gone the $0 funnel still favours the grounds (+4 hidden, +1 decoy). The eval's remaining precision gap to minus-grounds (0.828 against 0.893, recall 0.48 against 0.50) is two lookalikes delivered beside expected items (`large-vendor-freeze-1`, `week-1`), no false fire: the gap D112 leaves to the reader. No live Pi run was made (none was in this change's budget). | **Eval, no model:** precision 0.800 → 0.828, recall 0.48 → 0.48, falseFireRate 0.0147 → 0 (falseFires 1 → 0; scheduling precision 0.818 → 0.900); minus-grounds 0.893/0.50/0. **Eval `--live`** (`OpenRouterModel.fromEnv` with `EDEN_MODEL` unset: `z-ai/glm-5.3-flash`, the eval's planner and judge): 0.921/0.70/0.0147 → 0.923/0.72/0.0147, $0.0000070 → $0.0000067 per turn; the live false fire on `people-and-days-4` comes from the judge (`model:` reason) on main and here, and the shipped servers wire no judge (D112). **Funnel** (train1–6, seeds 41–42, cards replayed, reader stub): 214/211 against main, hidden +0 −0, decoys +0 −0; minus-grounds 210/210. | D120 | 2026-11-03 | The packed index's merges are incremental, and a purge no longer rewrites it (amends D111; may collide with a parallel branch's number, renumber on merge). At the reference's compaction points a purge frees the dead docs' numbers, reports their freed terms and drops them from the delta; their frozen postings stay, stale (scoring already skips a doc that is neither frozen nor live), until a merge filters them. A merge starts when the delta fills, or at a purge that leaves stale frozen postings with none under way; it seals the delta and does about 8,192 units of work (a posting each; zeroing the new arrays counts) per add or remove, in phases (count, prefix sums, allocate, copy, write the sealed docs', the forward arena), then swaps the new tier in; a merge within one step's work finishes in the add that starts it. Until then queries read the old frozen tier and the whole delta, sealed part first, so insertion order and every result are unchanged; a reused doc number becomes frozen only in a merge that filtered its stale postings. `SparseIndex.settle()` ends a merge at once: the engine's expiry tick calls it for every user, after the sweep (whose removes may purge and start one; `cache.test.ts`), since an index left mid-merge holds the old and new arrays together; `flush()` also merges, so nothing of a revoked source stays. A per-doc postings count (4 B) keeps the reference's purge counter exact. | D111's packed index paused about twice as often as the reference under churn, its worst step up to 40 ms, because delta freezes came on top of purges, each a pass over every posting. `index-memory.bench.ts`, churn at 6,892 items, 5,000 steps, three runs: steps over 1 ms D111 13–15 (14 on this VM before the change), reference 7–10, now 3–7 (after the review's pacing fix, three more runs: 4, 5, 7); median of those 6.0–9.5 ms (D111), 5.1–5.8 ms (reference), now 1.2–1.9 ms; worst step 21–44 ms (D111), 10.2–11.9 ms (reference), now 1.8–4.3 ms; step p50 unchanged, p99 63–104 → 340–394 µs (the merge's share, 0.1–0.4 ms, in about one step in ten). Index heap at rest unchanged (415 / 799 / 922 / 518 B per item at 6,892 / 2,000 / 500 × 20 / 100 × 200 items; D111 414 / 802 / 903 / 529); an index left mid-merge holds both copies (814 B per item for the 6,892-item build, which ends in one) until its next write or the expiry tick. `churn.bench.ts` (default `releaseTerms` on), main → here: worst put 19.84 → 5.07 ms, p99 9.48 → 2.44 ms, p50 0.410 → 0.388 ms, heap churned 10.93 → 11.03 MB, fresh 10.17 → 10.26 MB (after the review's fixes: worst put 5.39 ms, p99 2.79 ms, p50 0.395 ms, heap 11.0 against 10.3 MB fresh). `sparse-index-ab.bench.ts` within noise. Identical results: the randomized differential test against `sparse-index.reference.ts` passes, with a new heavy-churn mirror (merges in flight, purges, doc and term reuse, flushes, two seeds) and a test that a purge leaves the frozen tier as it is and a merge spans several adds; `bun run funnel` (ALL3 default, train1–6) equal to main on every per-task field but timings. |

It is polled anyway after 24 h with no notification (a lost one is bounded) and by the daily sweep; sources that do not notify are polled as before (D57, D63, D101 unchanged for them), and a notification for one is ignored (`synced: false`). (4) No change for prompt-cache placement: Pi's provider layer sends Eden's packet after the cached system prompt and tools; the lower cache-read share with Eden is fewer agent turns. | D133 | 2026-11-05 | **Freshness doubt is judged by read marks, not read times (amends D126; deep audit 2026-11-05 `fresh-readat-race`, commit c9ee1f304, and round 2 `d133-no-decision-row`; may collide, renumber on merge).** `SyncManager` keeps a global count of change signals taken (`Sync.mark()`); each source records the mark its latest signal set (`noticedMark`, replacing `noticedAt`). Every read takes the mark before its call and stores it with its copies (`ItemCache.put`'s `readMark`, `Entry.readMark`): slow-path lookups and probes at their start, re-reads before the fetch, sync backfill, poll and sweep pages before each listing call, `ingest` under the mark its signal set. A copy is doubted when `signalledSince(readMark)`: `noticedMark > readMark`. An unchanged copy read again keeps the later mark. So a signal while a call is on the wire doubts what that call brings, and items a notification carried or a poll the signal started do not; the round-1 proposal (a 1.5 s slack after each signal) is not used because it doubted those too. `readSlackMs` and `signalledAfter` are deleted. Evidence: commit c9ee1f304's message (quality-cli `--users 8 --sessions 20 --updates 0.6 --deletes 0.05 --push signals` identical to main apart from timings: freshness.checked 575, staleDelivered 1, deletedDelivered 0; the signal-during-fetch repro re-reads and answers with the new text; a mutation storing the mark at the read's end fails it); re-run in round 2 on the integration tip 006b1694b: `bun packages/eval/src/cli.ts` precision 0.8276, recall 0.48, falseFireRate 0, and `bun test packages/engine/src/freshness.test.ts` 15 pass. Known hole, fixed by round 2 package R2-P1: a lookup that joins a fan-out call begun before a signal stores the pre-change copy under its own later mark (`fresh-joined-flight-unmarked`); joined outcomes must carry the leader's mark. |

Changing N reshuffles users (review of the first attempt): their in-memory state starts over, `/v1/forget` goes to every child (503 unless all succeed) because rows written under the old N sit in another child's file, and source-map files no process of the new N opens are merged into the current set at start and deleted (`rehomeSourceMaps`), so no file holds rows forget cannot reach. `/healthz` stays 200 while any child is up, so one dead child does not take the instance out of a load balancer. **Outcome:** the trial ended promoted; `EDEN_PROCESSES` defaults to the core count (D178). |

An inverted index over `@eden/primitives` (`Vocab` ids) ships in the same package only if the bench shows at least 1.5x more at 40,000 docs over the term sets with no more memory; otherwise its numbers are recorded and it is not built. **Outcome (commit cdbdec3cb): the inverted index shipped.** `bun packages/sources/bench/static-source.bench.ts`, median of 3, per-search ms: at 4,000 docs old 38.3, term sets 2.34, index 0.351 (109x old); at 40,000 docs term sets 34.3, index 2.64 (13x) at 246 against 4,575 heap B per doc (about 1/19), so D175's bar (at least 1.5x over the term sets with no more memory) is met. The first search at 40,000 docs builds the index in 597 ms. |

| D220 | 2026-11-07 | **The secret gate redacts a bare New Relic user key (`eden-new-relic`) and the signature of a presigned or signed object URL (`eden-presigned-url`); this reverses D187's note leaving presigned URLs alone** (RealUse-1 weak point 10, session S6). `NRAK-` plus 27 letters or digits, any case, with no vendor name in reach (gitleaks' rule needs one). In a URL query, the value of `X-Amz-Signature`, `X-Goog-Signature` and `X-Amz-Security-Token` becomes `[REDACTED]`, the rest of the URL staying so it still names the object: after `?`, `&`, `;` (`&amp;` in HTML), `\u0026` (JSON from Go servers, whose encoding/json escapes `&`), `%3F` or `%26` (a URL percent-encoded inside another), with `=` or `%3D`, 16+ characters up to the next `&`, `%26`, `#`, quote, backslash, `<`, `>` or space. Why reverse D187: a SigV4 presigned URL is a bearer capability for up to 7 days (its `X-Amz-Expires` cap), granting a read or a write of a private object to whoever holds it, and Eden stores, indexes and sends to a model the text it gates; an expiry does not make that safe. The cost is a dead link in Eden's copy only; the source still holds the live one. Both have literal prefilters (`nrak-`; `x-amz-`, `x-goog-`), are swept by `redos*.test.ts` and attacked at 128 KB (and at 1 MB of `%` in one value) in `redos.test.ts`. The New Relic rule is one regex; the presigned rule finds the parameter name by regex and reads the value by a walk over its characters, each walk ending where the next name can begin. Two regex forms of the value were not linear on JavaScriptCore and were dropped in review: a lookahead at every character (240 ms per MB on one long value) and an unrolled loop over `%` (215 ms for a 1 MB value of `%`, and past that no match at all, so the value stayed in clear). Not covered: CloudFront and SigV2 `Signature=` and Azure SAS `sig=` (generic names, they need an anchor such as `Key-Pair-Id=`, `AWSAccessKeyId=` or `sv=` in the same query), other New Relic key kinds, a Sentry DSN (public by design). | `secrets.test.ts` (failing first; the JSON-escaped and percent-encoded shapes were found by the reviewer). The privacy probe (`packages/sim/lab/realuse-1/D/probes/privacy-probe.ts --reps 3`, 4-core shared box): the original 50 shapes 48 → 49 redacted (only `sentry-dsn` left); 5 presigned rows added (S3 signature, S3 session token, GCS signature, JSON-escaped, percent-encoded) 1 → 5; 30 original 1 MB crafted shapes worst `sanitizeForStore` 77.3 before, 68.3-77.1 after (noise), 3 shapes added (final rules: `presigned-param-repeat` 17.8 ms, `nrak-near-miss` 58.7, `presigned-encoded-repeat` 12.8; worst of the original 30 68.3); size scaling 0.25/1/4 MB on the final rules (ms per MB): one long signature value 9.5/9.7/8.9, one long value of `%` 15.5/5.8/5.8, `%26`-joined parameters with no stop 14.9/12.2/14.0, the encoded run 11.9/10.9/7.6, the JSON-escaped run 46.1/47.8/55.5, the NRAK run 56.6/56.1/46.5, `password-eq-repeat` 62.0/61.3/74.9: linear. `gate.bench.ts` 2 KB mean 12.6/19.9 µs before (two runs) against 12.8/10.4 after, 16 KB 119/130 against 105/128: within noise. docs/research/fix-s6-privacy.md. |
| D221 | 2026-11-07 | **One line sanitiser for every packet, answer and prompt line made from source or event text (`safeLine`, `safeBlock` in `packages/engine/src/line.ts`), and RealUse-1 part D's injection probe as a standing test** (RealUse-1 weak point 10, session S6). The forged-line bug (a line break in a title starting a line of its own that poses as another item, source or candidate) appeared in three places on its own: render.ts's packet, the MCP answer's Sources block, the judge's prompt. `safeLine` removes C0/C1 controls (U+001C–U+001E are line breaks to Python's `splitlines`) and bidi embeddings, overrides and isolates, collapses every whitespace run and line break (LF, CR, CRLF, VT, FF, NEL, U+2028, U+2029) to one space, also removes the direction marks (U+061C, U+200E, U+200F) and the Unicode tag characters (U+E0000–U+E007F, invisible to a person and read by a model), escapes `<` and its full-width and small look-alikes (U+FF1C, U+FE64) as `&lt;`, and trims; it is idempotent. Zero-width joiners stay (emoji). `safeBlock` applies the same rules but keeps lines, every break written as one LF, for fenced multi-line text (the judge's conversation). Now used by the judge prompt (whose candidate text kept its line breaks, so a candidate could forge a `[n] (source)` line: fixed) and `mcp.ts`; `render.ts` and `reader.ts` move to it when their owners' changes land (listed in the note). `injection.test.ts` runs 20 payloads (part D's 18 plus VT/FF and NEL/CRLF in a title) through observe, ask and the judge prompt in `bun run check`. | Tests first: `line.test.ts` (16, with a 2,000-string random check), `regressions.test.ts` (the judge's candidates and conversation; the candidates one failed before the fix), `mcp.test.ts` (controls and bidi in a Sources line failed before). `injection.test.ts` fails when the judge fix is reverted and when render.ts's NEL handling is reverted (checked by mutation). `bun run eval` unchanged (precision 0.8276, recall 0.48, falseFireRate 0, 0 leaked, 0 of 30 not inert); the default wires no judge, so no shipped prompt changed. |

(2) D205's revision notices: a packet queued untaken when its item is revised is checked at hand-out (`watchCopies`): a copy the cache has since replaced is followed by an "updated:" packet, also in a session that was handed nothing before; a packet the push's second reading withdraws announces nothing (`revision-notice.test.ts`, `push-behind.test.ts`); `takePending` orders its batch by which copy is current, not by packet kind: a packet with a copy the cache has since replaced goes first, packets rendering current copies after, updates after the other packets among the rest (with no cached copy to compare, an update is still the newest word), and an update a current packet of the batch covers is dropped, so the agent's last word on an item is its current copy, sent once in either queue order. | D310 | 2026-11-07 | **Server input edges under abuse (A11; amends D100 and D163).** (1) A body is scanned byte by byte before `JSON.parse` (`bodyShapeUnder` in `@eden/protocol`): more than 1,010,000 values (each `[`, `{`, `,` outside strings) or nesting past 1,008 is a 400, since what costs a parse is structure, not bytes; no valid request comes near either (a tool input holds at most about 500,000 values, D100). The router's `userOf` skips the parse for such a body and lets the child answer the 400. (2) The router holds its own GCRA at the tenant's undivided rate and burst and answers 429 with Retry-After before reading a body (D163 had it answer only `/healthz`, 413 and 503), so a tenant past its rate no longer gets unlimited body reads and parses on the thread every tenant shares; each child still holds 1/N. (3) `main.ts` stops on SIGTERM/SIGINT (and the router's disconnect) with the source map flushed and exits 0: as PID 1 in the image it ignored SIGTERM (10 s to SIGKILL), and a router child lost its pending outcome batch. (4) `.dockerignore` patterns gain `**/`, so a nested `.env`, `.local/` or Eden data directory is not copied into the image. | 8 MB bodies under the old path, best of 3: one string 4-7 ms, 4 M numbers 106-134 ms (served 200), 2.8 M empty arrays 265-339 ms, 4 M nested arrays 453-665 ms of parse on the loop; after, a 400 in under 100 ms, the scan about 9 ms on an 8 MB string body. A stalled body past a tenant's rate: before, no response and the connection cut at 12,017 ms; after, 429 in under 2 s, the other tenant 200. `docker stop`: 10.17 s (exit 137) before, 0.14 s (exit 0) after. Tests: `http.test.ts`, `router.test.ts`, `router-processes.test.ts`, `validate.test.ts`, `main-signal.test.ts` (each failed before). docs/research/a11-bugs.md. |
| D311 | 2026-11-07 | **The reports read only the engine's traces; a push's trace names its request's turn (A11; amends D277, D279, D293).** (1) `trace-report` and `digest` share one strict reader (`readTrace`): a line is a trace only if its decision is one of the engine's (`TRACE_DECISIONS`), its ids are strings and its counts finite and non-negative; a silent reason outside `SILENT_REASONS` prints as "other", and a connector id that is not `isId` as "unknown". A log is often stdout and stderr together, and a connector's stderr line shaped like a trace put its words ("Dana's salary is …", Markdown) into both reports, a string count concatenated (`"5"` gave a $1,806 row), and `1e999` printed a p95 of Infinity. A connector id with a colon is named whole. (2) An ask is its own event in "spoke" (an ask-only MCP session stays at turn 0, so its asks collapsed into one), and an explicit outcome stays the digest's last word for an injection whatever the lines' order. (3) With no user requests in the log the digest prints raw counts and "n/a" per 1,000 requests, not $0.00. (4) The `reader-push` trace and its span carry the turn of the request that started the push (`AskInternal.turn`), not the turn current when it settled: `/v1/explain` showed a push under a later turn, and the reports could count one request twice. (5) `explain`'s copy copies plans too. | `digest.test.ts`, `trace-report.test.ts`, `push-trace-turn.test.ts` (expected turn 1, got 3), `explain.test.ts`: each failed before. `bun run eval` unchanged (traces are not scored). |

At the expiry tick after a user has been neither read nor written for `freezeIdleMs` (counted in ticks, at least two: 20-30 minutes after the last use at the defaults; so a lookup that read the user's vocabulary before an await never finds it rebuilt), `ItemCache` replaces the user's vocabulary, index, sketcher and entry objects with one zstd (level 3) JSON blob of their entries (item, fetch time, read mark, class, raw hash, sketch, insertion sequence) and tallies, in memory: nothing goes to disk (D8). `revision` and `syncedCount` read the frozen record; every other read or write thaws: the same items in the same order, docs re-indexed in their old insertion order (`Entry.seq`, so equal scores reach the top-k in the same order), the same revision, probes, party and offset tallies (in their map order) and sketches. On the timer, freezes and the expiry of frozen users are background jobs of one slice scheduler (`pump`: one job at a time, ~5 ms a turn of the event loop); `observe`, `ask`, `checkAction`, `sourceItems` and a notice's revision poll, the server before a removal, and the sync before a due sweep or a backfill await (a bare change notice reads no item and leaves the user frozen) `cache.ready(scope)`, an urgent job of the same scheduler, so returning users queue rather than stall other requests (each awaits only when a thaw is pending: an extra await on these paths moves the funnel). | D327 | 2026-11-07 | **Trial `EDEN_SPEND_LEASES` (off): a tenant's daily model-spend cap holds across router children without being divided by N (amends D163 for model spend, while on)** (A14; A4's blocker 10). The router keeps one ledger per tenant and UTC day (`SpendLedger`); each child (`LeasedSpend`, the shared `TenantSpend` of its model roles) may spend a start of cap/(4N) before hearing from the router, and asks over IPC for another chunk of cap/(4N) once less than half a chunk is left; the router grants what the cap leaves (the N starts counted from the day's first lease). So all children together never pass the cap, and one busy child reaches at least 3/4 of it instead of 1/N. A restarted child spends its start again (at most cap/(4N) a restart); grants held by an exited child stay counted. The rate limit is still divided by N. | `spend-lease.test.ts`: four children, one busy: $20.31 of $25 (the most the other three starts leave) against $6.25 divided; all four busy: never past the cap; tenants' ledgers apart (D9); a new UTC day starts again and a late grant for the day before is dropped; with no router answering a child spends its start and asks once; `router-processes.test.ts`: the children hold cap/(4N) each through a real router. | D347 | 2026-11-07 | **A change notice may name the refs it changed, without their content: Eden reads those refs itself and puts nothing else of the source in doubt (`sourceRefs`, `POST /v1/sources/refs`, `EdenClient.sourceChanged(id, { refs })`, Python `source_changed(refs=…)`; A18).** For integrations whose notices carry ids but not content (a Gmail `history.list`, a Graph or HubSpot webhook, a Drive changes list). Each ref (at most 100 a call) is fetched in the background, two at a time, within `REF_TIMEOUT_MS` (5 s: off the agent's path, so it waits out a hosted connector's tail), a guard's skip or a plain error tried again (up to 4 times, 150 ms × k apart), and stored as synced under the read mark its call began with (D133), after `sourceItems`' removal check (D160, D176); a ref the source no longer has is dropped. Until its read lands the named item is in doubt (`refreshing`), and a delivery waits on that read rather than make a second call (at most `verifyTimeoutMs`), unless a signal came after the notice (then it reads the item itself: that read may predate the newer change). A landed read that changed the item is noted as a recent re-read in the user's live sessions, so a pool built before it goes out as it found the item. The first ref that cannot be read raises the plain signal before that item's doubt is lifted, so the source's copies are re-read on delivery as under D126; a forgotten user's reads stop; the stored ref is the one the gate left (D10). A source not synced and notifying gets the signal's answer and `reason`. The review's probes (a failure while other reads run, a signal during a read, a secret-shaped ref, a forget, a pool from before the read) are tests in `source-refs.test.ts`. | D351 | 2026-11-07 | **A key-less request from another site's browser page is refused (A20; amends D280, the dev mode of D9).** The server's dev mode (`EDEN_DEV=1`, the README's quickstart, with real sources) and the proxy with no `EDEN_PROXY_KEY` (its loopback default) serve whoever reaches them. A page the user visits reaches 127.0.0.1 too: as a blind cross-site POST (a `text/plain` body is parsed all the same, so it can report events into a named user's session), or by DNS rebinding (its own hostname re-pointed at 127.0.0.1, so it reads the answer as same-origin: `/v1/ask` returned the user's calendar text to a page whose Origin was `http://evil.example:8790`). Every browser POST carries `Origin`, and no agent's HTTP client sends one, so a key-less request whose `Origin` is not this machine (`localhost`, `127.x.x.x`, `[::1]`, any port; `null` refused) gets a 403 (`keylessOriginAllowed`, `@eden/protocol`): in `createHandler`, in the router (its children see only the key and content type), and in the proxy before anything is read or forwarded. Keyed requests are unchanged: the key is the check, and a browser cannot send it cross-site without a CORS grant Eden never gives. No configuration: a key-less caller on another origin should use a key. "Key-less" is the parsed key (`keyOf`, `auth.ts`), not the header's presence: the review found `Authorization: Bearer` plus a no-break space parsed to no key (dev) while the header was present, so the first version skipped the check for it and a rebinding page could read `/v1/ask` (a same-origin fetch may set `Authorization` with no preflight). | `browser-origin.test.ts` (3 foreign origins: 200 with the calendar text before, 403 after; loopback pages, no Origin and keyed requests 200; three `Authorization` values that parse to no key: 200 on the first version, 403 after), `router-processes.test.ts` (dev router: 200 before, 403 after), `proxy.test.ts` (2 foreign origins: upstream reached before, 403 and nothing reached after). |

| D425 | 2026-11-10 | **Frozen users may be persisted to disk, encrypted, as an operator opt-in (C3; departs from D8).** D8 holds item content in memory only, so "a restart costs warm-up, not a leak". With both `EDEN_PERSIST_FROZEN_DIR` and `EDEN_PERSIST_KEY` set (off by default; neither alone starts), a user frozen while idle (D325) is also written to disk: the same zstd blob of their already redacted entries (D10: items are redacted before they are cached, unchanged), their probes and the sync's state for them (D426), AES-256-GCM with a random 12-byte nonce per write, under keys HKDF-derived from the operator's 32-byte key (never logged; `PersistKey` prints `[redacted]`), the header (format, key id, time written) and the tenant and user ids as additional data, so a file renamed to another user's name or edited does not open. One file per user named by an HMAC of tenant and user (the directory names no one), 0600 in 0700 directories, written to a temporary file and renamed. What departs from D8, exactly: redacted item content (and the sync's ids, a user's probe queries and requested names) of users idle 20-30 minutes or more is on disk, encrypted, until the user is next used (a thaw deletes the file: what is on disk is only ever a frozen user's state), forgotten (`/v1/forget`, of the user or one source), has items removed, is evicted or expires, or the file is 7 days old (the cache's longest TTL); since D428, also once its newest item has expired. A file of another format (`PERSIST_FORMAT`), another key, past 7 days or that does not decrypt is deleted and reported by reason and count only; the user re-syncs. The directory is capped (`EDEN_PERSIST_MAX_MB`, default 4,096): a freeze past it is not written. Why opt-in: it trades D8's "nothing on disk" for restarts that do not re-read every source; the key beside the files means a host compromise reads persisted users, which an operator must choose, not inherit. A deployment setting, not an engine behaviour: no trial row (D112 governs `DEFAULT_CONFIG`, which is unchanged). | `restart.bench.ts 200 1000` (20-80 ms connectors, two runs): every user synced again in 42.87 / 44.00 s without, 14.08 / 14.20 s with; listing calls 2,400 → 600, items listed 200,000 → 3,000. | D426 | 2026-11-10 | **A user read back from disk takes back their sync state, conservatively (C3).** `SyncManager.snapshot` saves per source the synced ids, phase, cursor, cut, frontier, last poll and sweep, missed ids, change-rate posterior, used flag and sketch (`TermSketch.dump`), plus the user's requested names and used sources; `restore` installs it only when this process does not sync the user yet, so their sources are warm and the next lookup polls newest first instead of a backfill. What no process saw is treated as a signal: every restored copy has read mark 0, and each notifying source (its `notifies` and `fetches` saved and restored, so this holds from the first event, before any poll) gets a fresh signal mark and is behind until polled, so its earlier copies are re-read before delivery (D126); a user already synced here when read back keeps its state, its sources marked signalled the same way. Sources the user no longer has (removed while they were on disk, where no tick saw it) are dropped with their synced items before the returning event is served (`SyncManager.reconcile`, which `ready` waits on, bounded by `sourceTimeoutMs`). A connector's page token is saved only if `sanitizeForStore` leaves it unchanged (D10), else that source's next page is read from the head. A restored user gets a new cache revision, and the cache's sequence counter moves past its entries (equal scores keep reaching the top-k in insertion order, D325). With a model key, restored items are enqueued for cards again (cards are not persisted): the same card calls a re-sync makes. | `persist.test.ts` "the sync's state comes back": 10 listing calls to sync before, at most 2 after the restart, the planted item still delivered; without persistence the same restart lists 10 again. The branch's review found the first two gaps (a restored notifying source not doubted until its first poll; a removed source's items served on the first event); their tests fail without the fixes. |
| D427 | 2026-11-10 | **Persisted users under the router (C3; D163's rule for per-user files).** Each process writes its own directory, `<i>-of-<N>` under `EDEN_PERSIST_FROZEN_DIR` (one process: `0-of-1`), and holds 1/N of `EDEN_PERSIST_MAX_MB`; the router (or a process with no router) deletes every other N's directory before any child starts (`prunePersistDirs`), since a file's keyed name cannot say which child owns its user: changing N re-syncs those users, as D163 already has it for memory. `/v1/forget` reaches every child (D163) and each deletes the user's file in its own directory, held or not. At SIGTERM a process writes its live users within 1.5 s (the router kills a child 2 s after its SIGTERM); a crash writes nothing more. | Server `persist.test.ts`: the pruning, the cap divided by N, and `main.ts` writing a live user at SIGTERM, counting it on the next start and deleting it on `/v1/forget`. |
| D428 | 2026-11-10 | **A persisted user's file is deleted when its newest item expires, not only at 7 days (C3; amends D425).** A final review found a user whose file is never read back after a restart kept looked-up items (24 h TTL) on disk, encrypted, for up to 7 days: only the age sweep removed it. The file's header now carries an expiry (f64, ms on the cache's clock): the latest of each class's newest item plus its TTL (lookups 24 h, synced 7 days) and of the newest probe plus 24 h (probes live as long as a lookup's item, D46), never later than `maxAgeMs` (7 days) after the write; a user holding neither is written already expired. The header is in the AEAD's additional data, so an edited expiry does not open; `headerProblem` (at open and at read) and `sweep` (on the cache's expiry tick, from the headers read at open, no body decrypted) delete a file past `min(expiry, written + 7 days)`, reported as `expired`. Header 36 → 44 bytes, `PERSIST_FORMAT` 1 → 2: files the earlier build wrote are discarded as `version` (those users re-sync). | Engine `persist.test.ts` (+4 tests, 17): a file whose items have all expired is deleted by the sweep and at open and not restored, one fresh item keeps a file to its own expiry (a synced item's to 7 days), an edited expiry fails to decrypt and never passes the 7-day cap, a format-1 file is discarded; three of them fail without the change. |
| D430 | 2026-11-10 | **`askBehind`: the explicit ask stops waiting on its poll and on live searches of cut copies** (C4, B1's next step). An ask's lookup runs `behind` as the push's does (D405): a warm copy answers as it stands, the on-demand poll and the cut copies' live searches run behind (through the planner, joining reads already in flight), and the ask waits `askBehindWaitMs` (0) for them. Each source answered from a copy not whole or not current gets its search's outcome, "local" once polled current, "searching" while the work runs, or "error"/"timeout" once it is done and the copy is still not current (`behindStatuses`; a failed poll is never an all-clear); "searching" sources are named ("Still reading …: anything there that fits follows as a later packet", not an all-clear). What the work then stores that the ask would cite from the sources still being read, and the agent was not handed, goes out as an `ask-behind:` packet at the next take (no second search, no reader call, re-read as strictly as the answer, D211; dropped after the next request); a cut copy no search was sent for is "timeout" at once. New protocol status "searching" (additive). | D475 | 2026-11-10 | **Trial (off): a cold tier for the synced copy (`syncCold`, `syncColdMaxItems` 30,000, `syncColdVerify`).** Items the user's `syncMaxItems` cap pushes out of the index are kept compressed in memory, per (user, source), behind a Bloom filter of their words (`cold.ts`), and the backfill reads each listing to its end while there is room; a source whose items are all held, indexed or cold, is whole (`Sync.complete`) and is answered here: a lookup brings back the cold copies that hold its words (`Sync.thaw`, no call) instead of searching the source live (D264). Cold copies keep the read time and read mark they were read under (`ItemCache.putBack`), so D126 and D356 doubt them as before, and one a lookup brought back from a source with no change feed and a fetch tool is re-read before it is delivered or cited (`syncColdVerify`: it stands in for a live search's find). A source the user starts to use has its cold copies re-indexed (the cards and the intent index read only indexed items). A source that lists by time, or with neither a fetch by ref nor a change feed, stays out (its cut copy is searched live as before). Item content stays in memory (D8); a restart under D425 brings such a source back cut. Measured: funnel at 995 sources hidden 77 → 76 of 96 on both seeds, calls a task 48 → 11; the user-day bench 919 → 384 calls a day at 5/5/90; at cap 300 with connectors that neither push nor have a feed, deleted delivered/cited 3/14 → 2/12 and stale 15/143 → 12/132, bought with 0.55 re-reads a turn (4/17 without the re-read). Costs: 10 MB of cold items for a 1,000-source user, about 3x Eden's CPU a user-day. | F1; f1-local-copy.md §2, §4; `cold.test.ts`. |
| D476 | 2026-11-10 | **Trial (off): change feeds (`syncDelta`; `Source.changes`, `ChangePage`, `SourceFanout.changes`).** A source that can say what changed since a cursor (a Gmail history, a Graph or Drive delta, a Calendar sync token) is polled by one delta read: new and changed items wherever they sit in the listing, and deletions (dropped as a deletion notice's are). The cursor is taken before the backfill's first page. A complete delta read vouches for every copy held as of its start: `signalledSince` is false for a signal at or before it, so with `syncPush` a change notice costs one delta read, shared by every doubted item of the source (`recheckByDelta`), instead of D126's re-read of each; anything the feed cannot settle is re-read as before (D126, D211 unchanged). The daily re-listing for deletions becomes one delta read (or D356's re-read for an unused source); the whole listing is read again about every six days, daily while a listing has missed an item; an expired cursor is replaced and the source re-listed. A cursor taken over copies already held (after an expiry, a failed first read, a restart) vouches for nothing until a whole listing begun after it has ended, and the vouch covers only ids the sync holds (the review). A lost notice on such a source is bounded by `syncMaxStaleMs` (6 h), not `syncPushMaxStaleMs`. Departs from D345 only in that a delta read, unlike a listing poll, is complete. Measured (quality-cli, signal-only notices, 128 deletions): feed-only connectors deleted delivered/cited 5/17 → 0/0, stale 22/124 → 10/132; push + feed re-reads 1,492 → 0 with stale 10 → 10, the same with 20% and 30% of notices lost. `McpSource` has no delta tool yet, so no shipped connector has a feed. | F1; f1-local-copy.md §3, §4; `delta.test.ts`. |

| D505 | 2026-11-10 | **Trial `sourceYield`: each of a user's sources earns its live reads.** Per tenant/user and source, in memory and content-free (`source-yield.ts`): a run of the live reads the source answered since one of its items last reached the agent (a packet or an ask's answer; it halves every `sourceYieldHalfLifeMs`, a week), and two pools a user, reads on no evidence and reads on the request's words alone, each counting the reads of sources with nothing used yet and how many of those sources then had a first item used. A warm synced copy is not read live for a request (no search of its cut copy behind, no probe, no on-demand poll of a cut copy; it answers from what is held) when its run is at `sourceYieldRun` (4) and the request names nothing it mentioned, or when nothing of it was ever used and the pool for the request's kind of evidence is spent (after 24 reads, the upper 90% bound of uses per read under `sourceYieldFloor` 0.05). A source the request names an entity of is always read; so is one that has ever had an item used, until its own run fills. An ask names a cut copy it did not search (status "unsearched", never "local", D127) and does not call the answer an all-clear. A read is booked when the connector answered, once a source a turn, for warm copies only; a whole copy keeps its poll. Unlike D390's budget nothing bounds a lookup: each source earns or loses its own reads. A per-source run alone did nothing (at 1,000 sources a source is read ~0.4 times in 16 tasks), a cap on named reads lost recall where sources are few (0.528 → 0.496), and pools that counted the user's used sources never emptied: all three screened and dropped. At $0 (995 noise connectors, 5% of connectors notifying): funnel 47.3 → 33.3 and 48.0 → 31.0 calls a task with no hidden fact or decoy moved; over a user's 8 days 57.2 → 13.1 a request, hidden 37/46 both; stale delivered 15 → 12, deleted 3/14 both; five sources 314/274, task by task identical. | N1; n1-calls.md §2, §3, §7; `source-yield.test.ts`, `source-yield-partner.test.ts`. |

## Trial switches

Each trial has an owner and an end date (D112). At the end it is promoted into `DEFAULT_CONFIG` (measured better or
neutral at no extra paid cost) or deleted with its code and tests. A trial past its date is a bug.

| Trial | Switch | Owner | Ends | What decides it |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Action firewall (D69, D72) | `actionCheck`, `POST /v1/check` | repo owner | 2026-12-03 | A blind Pi run of `-act-firewall` with control tasks flagged at most 0.05 (D72's bar); else the switch, `firewall.ts` and the route go |
| Packet guard line (D306) | `guardLine` | repo owner | 2026-12-03 | A10's suite (`injection-ab.ts`) on an agent model that follows AI-addressed or social-engineering injections (Haiku 5.5 followed 4 in 432): promoted with D307 if attack success falls with hidden facts not lower, else deleted with `GUARD_LINE` |
| Instruction quarantine (D307) | `quarantineInstructions` | repo owner | 2026-12-03 | The same run: promoted if instruction-shaped attack success falls with hidden facts not lower and a precision check on real mail text finds no material false positives (its $0 screen already shows 0/198 targets reaching the agent and no planted item touched), else deleted with `shield.ts`'s quarantine |
| tsgo as the merge gate (D113) | `scripts/typecheck.sh` | repo owner | 2026-12-03 | A month of `tsgo` and `tsc` agreeing on every merge |
